In our study, we present such a system and study its capabilities and the fixation policies that emerge from it. Our system, FoAViS, samples visual input by adopting the established log-polar foveation and accumulates information in a 2D spatial memory. Due to its 2D structure, the same memory can be read out globally for classification or densely for semantic segmentation, without any architectural change. Fixations are drawn from a 2D map by a policy trained concurrently with the task. We find that learned fixation policies select more informative, task-specific locations than non-learned heuristics. These policies also shape the task representations in a beneficial way: for both classification and segmentation, models trained concurrently with a policy outperform models trained with random locations, even when the latter are provided with learned scanpaths. Moreover, these scanpaths exhibit similarities to human gaze behavior by capturing aspects of empirical scanpath characteristics. Our work marks an important step away from standard passive, single-pass architectures, towards biologically grounded models of vision. It opens a path for studying goal-directed gaze behavior and information representation in the visual system.
  </details>

- **[TwinPast: Benchmarking Historical Evidence Use in Streaming Video Understanding with Evidence Contrastive Video Pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41983)**  `arXiv:2610.41983`  
  _Haotian Yang, Yong Chen, Cheng Peng, Haobo Wang, Zhongle Xie, Xuanru Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High accuracy on streaming video benchmarks does not establish whether a model uses visual history. Our review of OVO-Bench and StreamingBench finds that recent frames suffice for 32% of sampled historical questions, while models also answer many questions correctly from the question and options alone. We introduce TwinPast to test whether answers correctly follow changes in history, using 350 human-reviewed video pairs spanning five historical tasks. Each pair holds the recent video context, question, and ordered options fixed while changing critical historical evidence and the correct answer. Each video is evaluated independently, and PairAcc requires correct answers for both histories. We evaluate 34 model and input configurations. For Qwen3-VL-8B and DeepSeek-Flash, historical inputs improve performance on TwinPast, reversing the recent-frame advantage on existing benchmarks. The strongest evaluated streaming system reaches 49.14% PairAcc, compared with 66.57-91.43% for the evaluated general-purpose models. This gap persists across all five tasks. Case studies further show that models select states from the wrong time or contradict recorded event order despite retaining the relevant evidence. These findings highlight the need to connect historical memory and retrieval with reasoning that uses evidence according to the question's temporal references and event relations.
  </details>

- **[Open-Weight MLLMs Cannot Compare What They Cannot Name](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41841)**  `arXiv:2610.41841`  
  _Wenxin Su, Anna Kreshuk_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly capable of understanding images and performing complex visual reasoning, suggesting the possibility of using them to automate biological image analysis. This need is becoming increasingly urgent as advances in technology continue to generate ever larger and more diverse microscopy datasets, already exceeding the scale at which manual visual analysis is feasible. With the final aim to use MLLMs for visual comparison of complex phenotypes, we investigate a very simple baseline: comparison of the size of body parts in synthetic images of ants. We find that state-of-the-art open-weight MLLMs fail for some body parts, but not for others, even though the comparison itself is visually trivial for all of them. We evaluate different in-context learning strategies and analyze performance on the related object recognition task. Our results suggest a recognition-driven grounding failure: structures that the models cannot recognize are instead associated with a recognizable semantic concept, impairing the grounding and comparison of both. Further analysis of the vision encoder is consistent with the hypothesis that fine-grained visual structure may be less well preserved without semantic support. Together, our results reveal a surprising limitation of current MLLMs: semantic recognition can gate simple visual comparisons that do not intrinsically require semantic understanding. Our code and data are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[ObjectGuidedMap: An Object-Aware Model for Robust Bird's Eye View Map Prediction in High-Occlusion Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41834)**  `arXiv:2610.41834`  
  _Christian Bohn, Harsh Yadav, Klaus Friedrichs, Hasan Tercan, Tobias Meisen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate BEV map prediction is a critical component of autonomous driving systems, enabling downstream planning and control. Recent approaches such as Mask2Map achieve strong performance by predicting vectorized, instance-level map elements directly from multi-view images. However, existing BEV map prediction models operate largely independently of object-level scene understanding, despite the fact that traffic participants frequently occlude or implicitly encode information about road geometry. In this work, we propose a multitask architecture that jointly predicts BEV maps and 3D objects, explicitly enabling information exchange between the two tasks. Building upon Mask2Map, we introduce an object detection branch and incorporate cross-task feature fusion at both the BEV feature level and the instance-query level via deformable attention and cross-attention mechanisms. This design allows map instances to leverage object context while remaining robust to occlusions caused by traffic participants. Experimental results on the nuScenes and Argoverse 2 datasets demonstrate that our approach consistently improves BEV map prediction accuracy, with particularly significant gains of 21% mAP in scenes containing heavy object occlusion.
  </details>

- **[A Patch is More Than a Token](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41814)**  `arXiv:2610.41814`  
  _Zixiong Wang, Haiping Ma_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Transformers represent each image patch through a canonical token produced under one observation condition. That token is a powerful interface, but it is still one realized state of the patch. We introduce PMTT, which establishes two foundations: a patch representation object can extend from one canonical state to a canonical-centered Representation State Family, and identity-preserving variation opens Patch-Level Local Feature Space. We develop four parts of this representation program. Patch Information Acquisition and Dense Orbital Views expose lawful same-patch states. Visible-to-Hidden prediction tests whether observed states reveal structure beyond themselves. Intrinsic analysis identifies low-dimensional, energy-concentrated, patch-specific, predictive geometry. Representation Organization separates acquiring more information from organizing that information, with an analytic Manifold as the central structured representation. We realize PMTT on frozen DINOv3 S/B/L/H+ backbones without neural post-training of the representation. Across two independent 4,096-anchor cohorts, Manifold explains 70.8-79.6% of Hidden variation, compared with 55.0-72.3% for directly Observed states, and wins on more than 95% of anchors at every scale. Effective rank falls from about 2.5 to 2.0 while dominant energy concentration rises with backbone scale. Across six visual capabilities and four scales, Observed exceeds the canonical Center in 21 of 24 conditions, Manifold exceeds Center in 23, and Manifold exceeds Observed in 16. The strongest current consequence appears in physical correspondence, where MegaDepth satisfies Center < Observed < Manifold at every scale. These results show that pretrained visual backbones contain richer patch information than the standard single-token interface directly exposes. Code and experimental assets are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Different Models, Shared Failures: Auditing Frozen-Encoder Pools for Medical Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41790)**  `arXiv:2610.41790`  
  _Eunseob Choi, Kyeonghun Kim, Hyuk-Jae Lee, Nam-Joon Kim_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Average segmentation accuracy and model count do not reveal whether models fail on the same cases. We present CoFailMed, an audit of correlated failures in pretrained-encoder pools for medical segmentation. Across five 2D tasks, we evaluate 11 public pretrained encoders (a controlled sample, not a census) using frozen features and separately trained $1\times 1$ readouts with four seeds per encoder. Shared-checkpoint pairs have the highest mean per-case Dice correlation. An expanded 17-encoder analysis shows that distinct checkpoints within the same encoder family remain more correlated than cross-family pairs. At the pooled 33rd-percentile Dice threshold, four-member pools drawn from distinct encoders have mean effective sizes of 1.56-2.92, estimated from failure correlations in pools with enough failures and passes per member. In the primary pool, richer readouts and fine-tuning narrow the Dice-correlation gap between pairs sharing a checkpoint (or the DINOv2-B/S release) and all other pairs. Yet dependence between different encoders can increase, and fine-tuning does not consistently increase effective pool size at 33rd-percentile thresholds recomputed for each training recipe. A smaller between-group gap can coexist with greater dependence. Pool evaluation should therefore report absolute dependence and threshold-specific effective size alongside accuracy and nominal pool size. The CoFailMed release provides aligned per-case traces, analysis scripts and study code to reproduce the central audit results.
  </details>

- **[Hard Sample-Conditioned Decorrelated Attention for Robust Face Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41789)**  `arXiv:2610.41789`  
  _Tahar Chettaoui, Guray Ozgur, Eduarda Caldeira, Naser Damer, Fadi Boutros_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-head attention in Vision Transformers (ViTs) allows independent attention patterns across heads, but without explicit regularization, heads may learn redundant patterns rather than complementary roles. Correlation-based penalties encourage head disentanglement, yet apply uniform regularization across all training samples. In face recognition (FR), hard samples whose embeddings lie close to competing identities are critical for optimization and generalization. Motivated by this, we propose ViT-HarDis, which conditions attention head disentanglement on sample difficulty: samples with high cosine similarity to negative class centers receive stronger head decorrelation, enforcing specialization where identity ambiguity is highest. Experiments show improvements over both the baseline and uniform disentanglement on most benchmarks. Our analysis shows that ViT-HarDis reduces the similarity of hard samples to competing class centers while decreasing inter-head attention similarity, and that applying disentanglement only to the final transformer layers outperforms regularizing the entire network. Trained on WebFace4M, ViT-HarDis achieves SOTA results among ViT-based methods on IJB-B, IJB-C, and TinyFace Rank-1, while achieving the best IJB-B and TinyFace results when trained on MS1MV3, improving FR without architectural modifications or additional inference cost. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[WorkIng: A Real-World and Synthetic Highway Work-Zone Benchmark Dataset for Autonomous Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41772)**  `arXiv:2610.41772`  
  _Xujun Xie, Dominik Rößle, Adithya Mohan, Daniel Cremers, Torsten Schön_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Highway work zones are critical edge cases for autonomous vehicles (AVs), combining atypical geometries and jurisdiction-specific regulations that existing benchmarks fail to capture: real-world datasets lack 360° multi-modal, full-scene 3D work zone annotation, and no single recorded site can span the layouts and regulatory variants needed for generalizable models. We present WorkIng, a dual-domain benchmark and training resource. WorkIng-Highway provides a 360° multi-modal work zone benchmark with synchronized 128-channel LiDAR, six RGB cameras, and RTK-GPS across 32 km, yielding 12,000+ annotated frames and 227,399 tracked 3D bounding boxes. WorkIng-CARLA complements this with procedural simulation spanning 24 regulation-aware scenarios across six canonical topologies and six international traffic standards, populated by 100+ custom 3D assets. Benchmarking representative 3D detection and end-to-end planning architectures shows that real-world data remains the primary performance driver, and that scenario-stratified synthetic augmentation improves architectures across 2D recognition, 3D detection, and trajectory planning. Resources:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Measuring What Matters: A Causal Reliance Score for Histopathology Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41763)**  `arXiv:2610.41763`  
  _Samuel Thomas, Daniel Tweneboah Anyimadu, Ahmed Karam Eldaly, Somaia Elsheikh, Mohammed M. Abdelsamea_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reliable histopathology segmentation requires models that rely on pathology-relevant structures rather than spurious background correlations. In this work, we argue that improving architectures alone is insufficient if models continue to rely on spurious correlations rather than pathology-relevant features. We introduce a causality-inspired evaluation framework based on controlled interventions that explicitly probes model sensitivity to foreground tissue structure and background/domain variation. Building on this, we propose the Causal Reliance Score (CRS), a metric that quantifies the relative sensitivity of model predictions to foreground perturbation versus background/domain variation under controlled interventions. To support this analysis, we design augmentation strategies that act as implicit counterfactual interventions during training, encouraging invariance to nuisance factors while preserving morphology. We demonstrate how these interventions reveal behavioural differences between models that are not captured by standard metrics such as Dice or IoU. While we instantiate our framework using a self-supervised transformer (CAUSE-ViT), the primary contribution of this work is not a new architecture, but a principled intervention-based methodology and evaluation metric for auditing model behaviour in medical image segmentation. Our results suggest that evaluating model behaviour through counterfactual interventions is essential for developing reliable and clinically robust medical image segmentation systems.
  </details>

- **[SAR-TS-BERT: Physics-Guided Self-Supervised Pre-Training With Polarimetric Features for SAR Time-Series Crop Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41728)**  `arXiv:2610.41728`  
  _Jieqiu Wang, Yuhan Zhang, Bo Zheng, Shaoming Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multitemporal synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery provides continuous, cloud-free observations of crop phenology for agricultural monitoring in cloudy and rainy regions, yet high-accuracy classification typically requires extensive labeled samples. Although self-supervised pre-training mitigates label dependency in remote sensing, existing frameworks primarily target optical imagery or generic spatial SAR scenes, underutilizing temporal backscatter dynamics and polarimetric scattering mechanisms. We propose SAR-TS-BERT, a physics-guided BERT-style self-supervised pre-training framework for SAR time-series crop classification. SAR-TS-BERT integrates backscatter intensities and polarimetric ratio features (VV, VH, and VV/VH) with dual-polarimetric Cloude-Pottier decomposition parameters ($H/\alpha/A$) into a six-band input representation through two core designs: 1) a physics-guided span masking strategy (SpanPhysics) that adaptively selects contiguous masked spans based on temporal backscatter change rates to prioritize key phenological transitions; and 2) a dual-branch cross-modal fusion encoder that models backscatter and polarimetric decomposition features through separate Transformer branches followed by bidirectional cross-attention fusion. On independent test sets in Estonia (EuroCropsML) and the USA (California CDL), SAR-TS-BERT achieves overall accuracies (OAs) of 96.78% and 87.29%, respectively, and attains a mean monthly OA of 99.37% on Chongming Island, Shanghai. On the Estonia benchmark, pre-training improves OA and Macro F1 by 1.17 and 1.59 percentage points over training from scratch under full supervision, and by 2.58 and 5.19 percentage points using only 1% labeled data. Incorporating SAR scattering physics into temporal self-supervised pre-training effectively enhances crop classification accuracy, parcel-level mapping consistency, and few-shot transferability.
  </details>

- **[Nereus: A Large-Scale Underwater Dataset for Fine-Grained Attribute Understanding and Grounded Counting Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41705)**  `arXiv:2610.41705`  
  _Jingtao Deng, Zihao Jin, Yuehe Chen, Feixiang He, Shuiwang Li, Dan Zeng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater imagery records rich ecological information and supports applications such as science communication, fishery monitoring, ecological conservation, and oceanographic research. Beyond general scene understanding, these applications require models to recognize fine-grained biological traits and reason over spatially grounded evidence. However, existing underwater datasets often provide coarse category labels, generic captions, or total-count supervision, which limits their use for organism-level analysis and ecological reasoning. We introduce Nereus, a large-scale dataset for Underwater Fine-grained Perception that jointly supports Fine-Grained Object Attribute Understanding and Grounded Counting Perception. Nereus contains 88K images and 2.68M question-answer pairs, including annotations for 136K marine organism objects across 2,690 species and 1,515 genera, as well as 65K point annotations and 67K grounded counting QA pairs. Experiments with representative multimodal models show that Nereus improves downstream adaptation over existing underwater-domain data and provides a foundation for future research on fine-grained marine perception.
  </details>

- **[Retrieval-Augmented Initialization with Dual-Guided Inversion for Flow-Based Inverse Problem Solvers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41695)**  `arXiv:2610.41695`  
  _Hansam Cho, Seoung Bum Kim_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Flow models have recently emerged as powerful generative priors for solving inverse problems. Despite their success, most existing flow-based inverse problem solvers initialize the reconstruction process with random Gaussian noise. However, recent studies in image generation have shown that the choice of initial noise can significantly influence generation outcomes. The role of initialization as a distinct design choice in flow-based inverse problem solving remains comparatively underexplored. In this work, we systematically evaluate noise initialization strategies while holding the downstream flow-based inverse solver fixed. We propose Retrieval-Augmented Initialization with Dual-Guided Inversion (RA-DGI), a method that constructs improved initial noise by leveraging retrieved proxy clean images and dual-guided inversion. Extensive experiments across multiple inverse problems demonstrate that the quality of the initial noise substantially affects reconstruction fidelity and perceptual performance. RA-DGI outperforms standard random initialization and achieves superior results compared to inference-time scaling strategies, while requiring less computational overhead. These findings highlight initialization as a critical yet previously overlooked factor in flow-based inverse problem solvers.
  </details>

- **[OT-CAL: Optimal-Transport-Guided Adaptive Sample Selection for Efficient Visual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41677)**  `arXiv:2610.41677`  
  _Suryansh Talukdar, Jin Soung Yoo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep neural networks for image recognition are typically trained using mini-batches, with a forward pass to compute predictions and losses followed by a more computationally expensive backward pass to update model parameters. However, not all training samples contribute equally to learning, suggesting that computation can be reduced by performing backward passes only on informative samples. The challenge is that sample informativeness changes throughout training: difficult samples may be valuable early on, improving samples become important later, and class diversity becomes increasingly relevant toward the end. Existing methods often rely on fixed selection rules or computationally expensive per-sample gradient calculations. We propose OT-CAL, an adaptive data selection method that automatically adjusts its sampling preferences during training. OT-CAL represents each sample using five informativeness features, including difficulty, learnability, and uncertainty. A curriculum vector describes the currently preferred sample characteristics, and optimal-transport-based matching assigns selection weights according to how closely samples match this target. The curriculum vector is then updated using the selected samples, forming a feedback loop that adapts without a manually defined schedule. Experiments on three standard visual learning benchmarks show that, when selecting 50% of each mini-batch, OT-CAL improves performance by 1.3--2.1 points over AdaSelection while achieving 1.18--1.24 times faster training than full-data training.
  </details>

- **[A Self-Supervised Pose Estimation Approach for Construction Machines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41673)**  `arXiv:2610.41673`  
  _Alaa Alshubbak, Daniel Görges_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pose estimation is a computer vision task used to estimate a skeleton of dynamic systems to predict future movements. Most of the research in this direction is based on a supervised learning approach which requires a massive amount of labeled datasets. In this paper, a self-supervised three-stage model based on a contrastive learning approach is introduced for estimating a skeleton of dynamic construction machines; such as excavators without using any labeled images for the first stage. The whole model structure is divided into three stages: the pre-train stage using the SimCLR contrastive approach, and two fine-tuning stages for the transfer learning and the downstream task. The model can leverage the features and learn from a huge unlabeled dataset called ACID to two small datasets generated from NVIDIA Isaac and MATLAB Simscape simulators as well as transfer the knowledge to a smaller dataset with a ratio of 3.5% from the original ACID dataset. The results show that the proposed approach can improve the accuracy of pose estimation for heavy construction machines in real images by 11% and 13% in comparison to the normal self-supervised approach with two backbones ResNet-50 and HRNet-W32, respectively.
  </details>

- **[When Depth Lies: Benchmarking Vision-Language Models on Mirror-Induced RGB-D Ambiguity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41651)**  `arXiv:2610.41651`  
  _Hao Yin, Tianchen Guo, Heming Du, Yan Ke, Yanbin Liu, Xin Yu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) have made significant progress in spatial reasoning, particularly by integrating RGB images with depth data, which is often assumed to faithfully reflect physical distances. However, mirrors violate this assumption: they distort depth readings while reproducing object appearances, making reflections difficult to distinguish from real objects. As existing benchmarks rarely consider such reflective scenarios, VLM performance in mirror scenes remains largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce a mirror-centric RGB-D dataset from reflective scenes to evaluate the spatial reasoning capabilities of state-of-the-art VLMs in these scenarios. Specifically, we collect 1,011 RGB-D images from 525 real-world scenes, spanning diverse indoor and outdoor environments, six reflective surface types, and both daytime and nighttime conditions. All images are annotated with reflective surface region masks and mask annotations for both directly observed and mirror-reflected objects. Then, we curate a benchmark named MIRA-Bench (Mirror-Induced Reasoning Ambiguity Benchmark), comprising 14,140 QA pairs across eight sub-tasks organized into three cognitive levels: Reflection-aware Perception, Spatial Reasoning, and Scene-level Decision-making. We evaluate state-of-the-art VLMs on MIRA-Bench and find consistent failures across tasks: models misclassify whether objects are directly observed or reflected, fail to match reflected instances to their real-world counterparts, and produce erroneous spatial inferences. Remarkably, providing depth data actually degrades reasoning performance, implying that VLMs cannot identify incorrect depth in mirror scenes. The code and data are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[TADP: Dual-Branch Temporally Adaptive Differential Privacy for Expression-Aware Video Anonymization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41642)**  `arXiv:2610.41642`  
  _Feng Xu, Xun Li, Lars Petersson, Yulei Sui, David Ahmedt-Aristizabal, Dadong Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present TADP, a temporally adaptive differential privacy (DP) framework for inference-time privacy-preserving video generation under fixed-length clip-replacement adjacency. TADP uses a dual-branch design to extract identity and expression representations, clips and privatizes both branches with temporally adaptive Gaussian mechanisms whose noise scales depend only on preceding privatized releases within the same clip and are bounded below by history-uniform calibrated minima, and accounts for their joint release via adaptive Rényi Differential Privacy (RDP) composition. The clip-level RDP guarantee is converted to $(\varepsilon,\delta)$-differential privacy, while soft quantization and subsequent temporal processing are applied only after privatization as DP post-processing. Experiments on facial expression datasets show reduced identity leakage under evaluated attacks, usable perceptual quality and temporal stability, and high facial expression recognition utility relative to the evaluated privacy-preserving baselines. TADP formally satisfies clip-level DP and empirically provides a favorable privacy--utility trade-off under the evaluated threat models and datasets.
  </details>

- **[Attention Maps Know When to Think: Training-Free Difficulty-Aware Chain-of-Thought for Reasoning Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41618)**  `arXiv:2610.41618`  
  _Chanseul Cho, Jaesung Jun, Dongheon Lee, Youngmin Ro_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting has become a standard reasoning strategy in Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs), showing strong performance in complex vision-language tasks such as reasoning segmentation. However, our analysis reveals that indiscriminate CoT usage can lead to degraded performance by inducing unnecessary reasoning, a phenomenon we term overthinking. In reasoning segmentation, overthinking often leads to misalignment between the query's intent and visual grounding. To address this, we propose Difficulty-Aware Chain-of-Thought (DA-CoT), a training-free adaptive framework that selectively triggers CoT based on the input's intrinsic difficulty. We find that the entropy of the model's attention maps reliably reflects task difficulty: low entropy indicates confident, localized grounding, while high entropy suggests uncertainty and potential benefit from CoT. DA-CoT uses this entropy signal to determine whether to apply CoT prompting. Additionally, we introduce a Bidirectional Rethinking mechanism that enables the model to automatically flip its reasoning decision when the initial output fails, enhancing robustness by recovering from failures induced by overthinking and underthinking. DA-CoT demonstrates its effectiveness by achieving state-of-the-art performance on the Reasoning Segmentation benchmarks while significantly improving inference efficiency.
  </details>

- **[Scout-Refine-Correct: Teaching Flow Models to Refine Their Own Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41598)**  `arXiv:2610.41598`  
  _Sergey Karpukhin, Aibek Alanov_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The quality of image generation models trained with diffusion or flow matching is largely fixed by the training budget: at inference, the only available lever is the number of sampling steps, and additional steps improve quality only up to a point. We present a training recipe, Scout-Refine-Correct (SRC), that instead gives a flow-matching generator a genuine inference-time refinement capability. At each sampling step the model takes a scout rollout -- $K$ steps ahead along its own trajectory -- extrapolates a full-sample estimate, and feeds that estimate back to itself as a self-conditioning signal before taking the real step. This can be repeated for $R$ refinement rounds. Two correction losses, applied during training at states the model's own rollouts actually reach rather than only on the ground-truth path, teach the model to use this signal to reach new samples instead of collapsing to a suboptimal one. On ImageNet-256, SRC improves LightningDiT-B from FID~14.5 to FID~7.26 at NFE~550, matching a privileged best-of-$N$ baseline that uses an oracle FID verifier to choose among samples. SRC uses no external verifier, no reward signal, and, after ablation, almost no extra parameters over the base architecture. Combining SRC with best-of-$N$ improves the result further, to FID~3.81 at NFE~4400, showing that the two axes are complementary. We ablate the main design choices -- the architectural pathway for the scout signal, the scout horizon, each correction loss, and the training schedule to demonstrate key working components of training.
  </details>

- **[TACO: Trajectory-Aware Curvature Observation for Memorization Localization and Mitigation in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41520)**  `arXiv:2610.41520`  
  _Ruxi Gu, Zilei Wang, Wei Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image diffusion models can reproduce memorized training content within localized image regions, making precise localization essential for targeted mitigation. However, curvature observations at a single denoising state can contain transient responses, while spatially uniform mitigation may disrupt prompt-relevant content. We present Trajectory-Aware Curvature Observation (TACO), a unified framework for local memorization detection and mitigation in diffusion models. Our empirical analysis reveals that memorization-related curvature becomes pronounced during late-stage denoising and persists at consistent spatial locations across neighboring states. Motivated by these observations, TACO aggregates prompt-dependent curvature responses along the late denoising trajectory and applies latent-guided spatial refinement to improve spatial coherence. The resulting memorization map serves both as a localization signal and as a spatial control signal for mitigation. Specifically, TACO uses this map to steer existing inference-time interventions toward memorization-related regions through spatial reweighting and localized guidance correction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TACO consistently outperforms competing localization methods, and improves the Pareto trade-off between memorization suppression, semantic alignment, and perceptual quality. These results establish trajectory-aware spatial analysis as an effective link between memorization localization and targeted mitigation.
  </details>

- **[VoxLution: Evolving Voxels for High-Fidelity 3D Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41501)**  `arXiv:2610.41501`  
  _Daoyi Gao, Xinyi Zhang, Alexey Bokhovkin, Lei Li, Angela Dai_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-resolution 3D indoor scene generation is crucial for immersive applications such as virtual reality, spatial computing, and robotics simulation. We introduce VoxLution, a coarse-to-fine transformer-based approach that generates high-fidelity indoor scenes with rich details, overcoming the overly smooth geometry typical of existing generative models. First, we generate a low-resolution spatial layout; then, based on this structural information, we progressively refine the scene geometry locally in chunks. Key to our approach is formulating 3D super-resolution as a sequence modeling problem over multi-resolution sparse voxel grids. To achieve this, we introduce a training-free, resolution-invariant tokenization scheme that compactly encodes the geometric information of eight octants into a single decimal number. We formulate the transformer sequence to include multiple resolutions, allowing the coarse layout to guide the generation of fine local details. To ensure coherence across local chunks, we incorporate adjacent chunk boundary information directly into the transformer sequencing. VoxLution achieves scene generation at a finer voxel resolution than existing baselines, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on standard benchmarks with 59.6% lower FPD and 51.1% lower FID on 3D-FRONT, and 70.5% lower FPD and 73.8% lower FID on Scannet++.
  </details>

- **[VariaFlow: Uncertainty-Aware Flow Matching for Unsupervised Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41494)**  `arXiv:2610.41494`  
  _Arnela Hadzic, Martin Urschler, Cosmin I. Bercea_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstruction-based anomaly detection no longer fails at reconstruction. Generative models produce high-quality pseudo-healthy reconstructions, and the remaining difficulty is the step of scoring residuals. The residual between a scan and its reconstruction has two indistinguishable sources, genuine pathology and the anatomical variation that separates one healthy brain from another. Scoring against a single reconstruction treats both as anomalous, inflating false positives where healthy anatomy varies most while small or subtle lesions are lost in the surrounding reconstruction error. We identify the missing quantity as the predictive variance, how much a healthy reconstruction can vary at each location. We introduce VariaFlow, which estimates this variance from an ensemble of mask-guided restorations under an unconditional flow matching prior, each started from a different noise initialization, and which normalizes the residual by the resulting standard deviation. Few-step flow matching keeps the ensemble computationally affordable, and restoring at two spatial scales captures lesions of different size. Across three brain MRI datasets spanning stroke and tumor, VariaFlow is trained once on healthy anatomy and generalizes across all three, attaining the highest AUROC (0.94, 0.80, 0.78) on each and the highest AUPRC on both cross-domain datasets, exceeding the strongest baseline by 37% and 50%. Under domain shift, the same baseline drops by up to 52% in AUPRC, whereas VariaFlow stays more stable and achieves the best small-lesion Dice on all three datasets.
  </details>

- **[BDNet: Fast-slow thinking is all you need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41485)**  `arXiv:2610.41485`  
  _Chenzhi Zheng, Zhuoran Zheng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present BDNet, a hierarchical image-classification backbone built around fast image features and slow statistical references. Its baseline deformation operator (BDO) transforms the deviation of a feature from a stage-specific reference through a low-rank projection and a learned channel scale. Rather than computing pairwise token similarity, the branch operates directly on baseline-relative residuals with complexity linear in the token count. Each of four reference buffers accumulates detached stage statistics during training and remains frozen at inference. A four-stage pyramid with depths $(2,2,6,2)$ and widths $(96,192,384,768)$ yields a 20.70M-parameter classifier at $224\times224$ resolution. We organize an ImageNet-1K study around clean classification, eight controlled ablations, and Gaussian-noise, contrast, and brightness perturbations. The central hypothesis is that a slow reference can improve the robustness of a compact representation while retaining a simple tensor implementation. We distinguish this training-time statistical mechanism from input-dependent attention and make its linear inference behavior explicit.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Trial-and-Error: A Geospatial-Guided Active Reasoning Framework for Robust Aerial Vision-and-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41446)**  `arXiv:2610.41446`  
  _Jiawei Ge, Wenkang Gong, Jiuxin Cao, Bo Liu, Lei Cui, Haoran Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Aerial Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) requires UAVs to locate language-specified goals in large-scale urban environments, where objects often appear small and visually ambiguous from high-altitude viewpoints. Existing methods largely follow a reactive paradigm common in indoor VLN, repeatedly updating navigation states from local observations while underutilizing the geospatial structure that remains stable during navigation. As a result, visually similar distractors can cause a reactive goal search to repeatedly reinforce locally plausible goal hypotheses based on passively accumulated evidence, leading to unreliable localization. To address this, we propose GeoReasoner, a robust framework that shifts the paradigm of Aerial VLN from reactive trial-and-error to geospatial-guided active reasoning. Specifically, GeoReasoner first performs pre-search geospatial reasoning to derive region-level priors and constrain the goal hypothesis space. Building on these, it further conducts hypothesis-driven exploration to actively acquire discriminative evidence and refine competing goal hypotheses. Finally, consistency-aware goal verification adversarially assesses complementary evidence sources before stopping, hence reducing premature or erroneous goal confirmations. Experiments on the real-world CityNav benchmark demonstrate that GeoReasoner sets a new SOTA among zero-shot Aerial VLN methods while competing favorably with supervised approaches.
  </details>

- **[FlexReader: Towards Adaptive Decoding Paths for Efficient Arbitrary-Length Scene Text Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41426)**  `arXiv:2610.41426`  
  _Zhibin Ma, Pengwen Dai, Zhigang Yao, Xugong Qin, Wenqi Ren, Xiaochun Cao_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing Scene Text Recognition (STR) models are inherently coupled with a specific decoding strategy by their training paradigms, limiting their flexibility in deployment across diverse recognition scenarios. In this paper, we propose FlexReader, an STR model that supports multiple decoding strides, enabling a single model to achieve an optimal accuracy-efficiency trade-off across different datasets. Specifically, we first introduce a Sub-string Perception module to perceive the target sub-string from visual features, followed by multiple parallel decoding blocks that independently decode each character in the sub-string. During inference, FlexReader supports decoding with arbitrary strides within the perceived sub-string range. Furthermore, we propose Adaptive Prefix Decoding, which adaptively selects the decoding stride at each step based on the model's confidence in the decoded characters, thereby identifying the optimal decoding path for each input. Experimental results demonstrate that FlexReader exhibits robust recognition capability across a diverse range of benchmarks, including those featuring long text, curved text, and occlusions. It consistently outperforms existing decoding strategies in recognition accuracy while achieving up to 6-flod decoding speedup over autoregressive decoding.
  </details>

- **[DUGO: Dual-Scale Gaussian Memory for Online Temporal Action Localization in Egocentric Procedural Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41402)**  `arXiv:2610.41402`  
  _Mohammed Aman Bhuiyan, Md. Sazzad Hossain Adib, Samiul Basir Bhuiyan, Mir Faiyaz Hossain, Syed Mohammed Shamsul Islam, Md Zakir Hossain, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Online Temporal Action Localization (OnTAL) aims to detect and localize action instances from untrimmed streaming videos in real time. Unlike offline approaches, OnTAL models have no access to future frames and cannot go back and refine earlier predictions, which makes the task considerably harder given the strict real-time constraints and narrow temporal window available. Most existing OnTAL methods lean on either brief anchor-based context or a single-scale memory of past frames, which limits how well they can capture actions unfolding across multiple time scales or requiring long-range reasoning in Procedural Videos. To address these gaps, we present DUGO (Dual-Scale Encoding and Gaussian Memory Online), a new OnTAL framework that jointly models local motion dynamics and long-range temporal dependencies. At its core, DUGO uses a Dual-Scale Temporal Encoder, pairing depth-wise local representations with global self-attention so it can pick up both fine-grained temporal patterns and broader contextual cues. We also introduce a Gaussian Memory Bank, which represents action memory as stochastic, input-conditioned Gaussian prototypes, allowing for richer temporal memory through gated cross-attention applied at both the encoder and anchor-query stages. Extensive experiments on procedural egocentric datasets (EGTEA, EPIC-Kitchens-100) demonstrate that DUGO achieves state-of-the-art performance while also providing substantial improvements on non-egocentric benchmarks such as Muses. The project page is available at:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Parse Before You Execute: Efficient Spatial Reasoning with Executable Primitives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41389)**  `arXiv:2610.41389`  
  _Xiwei Liu, Yulong Li, Xinling Zhuang, Xuhui Li, Yichen Li, Haolin Yang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial reasoning in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) has increasingly benefited from tool-integrated pipelines that ground computation in explicit geometry. However, existing systems often compile queries into relatively open-ended artifacts, such as code, task constraints, tool plans, or gathered evidence, leaving what must be resolved before execution under-specified. This can lead to unnecessary tool acquisition and iterative refinement, while still producing plausible reasoning traces from incomplete spatial commitments. We identify this failure mode as under-committed query compilation. To address it, we propose PEPS, a training-free agentic paradigm Parsing Executable Primitives for Spatial reasoning under a typed FESM schema over Frame, Entity, State, and Metric. PEPS follows a parse-before-execute principle: the Parser commits each query to a compact set of executable requirements; the Executor selectively grounds the required primitives through fixed geometric APIs and deterministic computation; and the Verifier checks execution sufficiency, enabling early termination or targeted repair. Extensive experiments across challenging spatial reasoning benchmarks show that PEPS consistently improves reasoning accuracy while substantially reducing refinement rounds, API cost, and end-to-end latency over strong tool-integrated baselines. These results demonstrate that explicit requirement commitment can make tool-integrated spatial reasoning not only more reliable, but also more execution-efficient.
  </details>

- **[Queryable Gaussian View Index: Text Retrieval and Selective Camera Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41373)**  `arXiv:2610.41373`  
  _Tianyu Xiong, Rui Li, Jiaqi Yang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present Queryable Gaussian View Index (QGVI) for querying existing Gaussian scene assets and their associated calibrated reference data. Text-view retrieval and selective RGB camera localization return poses in the asset's coordinate frame. QGVI-T turns Gaussian-rendered views into a language-searchable index of scene locations, binding structured captions to known camera poses and place identifiers. Semantic and lexical retrieval select views from distinct places without online rendering, VLM calls, or a scene-specific pose network. The image route, QGVI-I, filters LoGS-based geometric estimates using distinct inlier query keypoints. It omits photometric refinement and preserves accepted pose coordinates. On nine development scenes, QGVI-T achieves 58.3% Top-1 and 78.3% Top-5 joint relevance-and-validity success, compared with 26.1% and 34.6% for our CityLoC reimplementation with refinement. QGVI-I achieves 97.3% scene-mean pose success at 1 m/2 degrees, with pooled false-acceptance and false-rejection rates of 0.707% and 1.142%. A paired cached evaluation retains all 1,775 initializer poses correct at this threshold. Recorded median text query latency is 0.020 s. Matched controls show competitive BM25 retrieval and slightly lower false acceptance for thresholded full LoGS; grid augmentation suggests modest gains on novel-view-derived descriptions. We report model-judged semantic success separately from source-camera recovery, with source-verified or estimated scales for geometric evaluation.
  </details>

- **[Task-Performance Routing for Multi-Teacher Distillation in CT Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41362)**  `arXiv:2610.41362`  
  _Jiaye Yang, Yao Liu, Die Dai, Hanwen Zhang, Peiyuan Jiang, Qiao Liu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pre-trained models offer complementary strengths in CT organ segmentation, but their relative accuracy varies across organs and spatial regions. Multi-teacher knowledge distillation can combine these strengths in a single student, yet global or instance-level teacher weights overlook variation within a 3D volume. We propose Task-Performance Routing (TPR), which uses region-wise segmentation errors to determine teacher weights during training without learned routing parameters. In hard regions, TPR favors teachers with lower prediction errors; in easy regions, it uses uniform weights to combine their knowledge. A hierarchical spatial partition provides fine-grained routing in shallow encoder stages and coarse-grained routing in deep stages. Experiments on BTCV, MSD-Liver, and MSD-Pancreas with two teacher pairs and two backbone architectures show higher Dice scores than the individual teachers, adaptive ensembles, and evaluated multi-teacher distillation baselines. On MSD-Pancreas, TPR achieves Dice scores of 65.40% with SwinUNETR and 68.25% with U-Net, compared with 64.38% and 67.45% for the corresponding adaptive ensembles. Inference uses the student alone, with no routing or teacher overhead. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[GenDSR: Transferring Representations from Video Generation Models for Dynamic Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41314)**  `arXiv:2610.41314`  
  _Ke Yang, Zhenyu Zhang, Jun Li_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dynamic spatial reasoning is essential for connecting visual intelligence to thephysical world, yet remains challenging for vision-language models (VLMs), which must preserve scene state while tracking how spatial relations evolve over time. Meanwhile, video generation models (VGMs), trained to synthesize temporally coherent world dynamics, offer a rich source of spatiotemporal representations. We present GenDSR, a framework that transfers intermediate representations from VGM into VLM for dynamic spatial reasoning. Rather than treating a VGM feature as a monolithic prior, GenDSR constructs two complementary branches: a raw branch preserving holistic generative context and a delta branch emphasizing temporal variation. Task-conditioned tokenwise gates then fuse both signals and inject the result into the native visual representation. On DSR-Bench, GenDSR achieves 67.2%, surpassing the previous best result and establishing a new state of the art.
  </details>

- **[STAR-FER: Adaptive Selective Restoration for Edge-Based Low-Resolution Facial Expression Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41310)**  `arXiv:2610.41310`  
  _ZhongYang Wu, ZhenFeng Gan, Di Zhao, XiaoJun Zhu, HouDe Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Variable interaction distances continuously degrade facial resolution in edge-based affective interaction, making low-resolution facial expression recognition (FER) difficult under limited computation. Full-face image or feature restoration incurs substantial overhead, whereas lightweight recognition pipelines may miss local cues that remain discriminative after degradation. We propose STAR-FER, a scale-and-task-adaptive selective restoration framework that balances recognition performance and resource constraints. A lightweight image-scale conditioned Gate jointly predicts a pixel-wise spatial ranking and a continuous restoration budget, then applies exact-support projection to obtain an input-dependent free-form mask. The mask-conditioned restorer retains complete facial context while concentrating sparse restoration and residual insertion on the selected support. Across seven degradation scales from x2 to x8 on the complete RAF-DB test set, STAR-FER achieves 80.92% mean accuracy and 71.64% Macro-F1, improving the low-resolution ResNet-18 baseline by 2.25 and 2.71 percentage points, respectively. It surpasses SR-FER by 1.04 accuracy points while requiring 8.84 GFLOPs, a 54.29% reduction, with 28.01% mean mask coverage, and maintains effective recognition at unseen non-integer scales. These results show that spatial-ranking and continuous-budget driven selective restoration reduces spatial modification and computation while maintaining low-resolution FER performance, offering an efficient path to resource-constrained affective interaction.
  </details>

- **[SketchDirector: Complex Scene Sketching via Agentic Layout Reasoning and Detail Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41260)**  `arXiv:2610.41260`  
  _Yiqi Liang, Xiaodong Cun_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing sketch generation methods often rely on image guidance and are largely limited to individual objects or simplified scenes, leaving text-guided complex scene sketching underexplored. We present SketchDirector, an agentic framework that mimics the human sketching workflow for automatic multi-concept scene sketching. Given a scene-level text prompt, SketchDirector first employs a large language model~(LLM) to decompose the scene into multiple draft assets, where each is represented by a set of Bézier curves. Then, an agentic reasoning loop iteratively plans and executes their spatial arrangement, guided by self-evolving memory. Finally, a detail refinement module leverages diffusion priors to enhance sketch quality while preserving geometric structure. To support future research and applications in multi-concept scene sketch generation, we further introduce the \textit{MCSketch8K} dataset, comprising editable vector assets, compositional layouts, and object-level manipulation annotations. Extensive experiments show that SketchDirector outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in semantic and compositional quality while effectively preserving overall contour organization during refinement. The project page is: \href{this https URL}{this https URL}.
  </details>

- **[ReVEAL: Eliciting Role-Aware Latent Visual Evidence from MLLMs for Reasoning Video Object Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41257)**  `arXiv:2610.41257`  
  _Zhongyuan Bao, Cunjie He, Lejun Zhang, Zenglin Xu, Yanwei Fu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reasoning video object segmentation (ReVOS) requires segmenting the target specified by an implicit query throughout a video. Existing MLLM-based methods typically require costly cross-model alignment, tying MLLM training to a downstream segmentation model. We present ReVEAL, a decoupled thinking-with-video framework for ReVOS that elicits role-aware latent visual evidence from MLLM reasoning to directly guide segmentation. ReVEAL introduces Visual Evidence Tokens (VETs) into video reasoning to associate necessary visual regions or entities with target, background, or distractor roles, while a Dynamic Attention Layer Router adaptively aggregates VET-to-video attention across layers into role-specific grounding evidence. We train the MLLM to perform VET-augmented reasoning on our constructed ReVEAL-47K dataset, with a Contrastive Attention Grounding Loss as auxiliary supervision to apply role-specific constraints to VET attention and optimize layer routing for reliable latent grounding. At inference, ReVEAL identifies the target through this thinking-with-video process and fuses the resulting role-specific evidence to guide a frozen SAM2 in predicting target masks. Experiments on five benchmarks show that ReVEAL outperforms alignment-free baselines and remains competitive with alignment-based methods.
  </details>

- **[Hawkeye: Reading Illegible Text with Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41254)**  `arXiv:2610.41254`  
  _Brian Tang, Kang G. Shin_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While modern Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have been remarkably successful in general scene text recognition, they consistently struggle with the long-tail distribution of text that is degraded by motion blur or low resolution. These limitations are particularly apparent in egocentric applications like smart glasses, where text is often captured in noisy environments. In this paper, we introduce the \textbf{BSTRB} (Blurry Scene Text Recognition Benchmark), a novel benchmark of 8,398 noisy image samples with high-fidelity annotations derived from 1,699 real-world video sequences. To support this, we present \textbf{Hawkeye}, a VLM fine-tuned on BSTRB to read and reason about illegible text by leveraging contextual priors and pixel-level alignment. Our results show that \name matches much larger VLMs on BSTRB overall and exceeds them on text at or below 20 pixels, in a single pass suited to real-time deployment. We characterize the resolution at which current VLMs begin to fail.
  </details>

- **[Coarse3D: Controllable and Faithful Sparse Structure Generation for Image-to-3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41231)**  `arXiv:2610.41231`  
  _Gaige Wang, Bowen Pan, Hao Ren, Jiangjing Lyu, Chengfei Lyu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Coarse-to-fine 3D-native generation relies heavily on the first-stage sparse structure, which determines the global orientation and overall geometry for subsequent refinement. However, existing coarse generators suffer from two key limitations: their orientation is difficult to control due to ambiguous semantic canonicalization, and their structures are often inconsistent with input images due to weak 2D-3D grounding. We propose Coarse3D, a sparse-structure generation model that produces orientation-controllable and structurally faithful voxel geometry from arbitrary unposed images. Coarse3D introduces three designs: a first-frame anchored canonical space that aligns generation with the first input view while preserving object-centric normalization; a dual-tower DiT that couples generation with auxiliary feed-forward reconstruction to learn spatially grounded features; and spatial-prior guided attention that regularizes cross-attention with pixel-to-voxel correspondence. Coarse3D can directly replace the coarse stage of existing pipelines. Experiments show that it substantially improves sparse structure quality and consistently enhances final 3D generation, achieving state-of-the-art performance.
  </details>

- **[From Evaluation to Evolution: Benchmarking Image Editing and Evolving Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41210)**  `arXiv:2610.41210`  
  _Jun He, Canmiao Fu, Ying Zhang, Zhipeng Huang, Chenyu Mu, Yazhen Xie, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-world image editing requires coordinating multiple visual transformations with content preservation and reference constraints, yet aggregate scores can readily obscure unmet local requirements. Effective evaluation must therefore identify local failures and translate them into priorities for subsequent model improvement. We introduce WeGenEditBench, comprising 2,740 evaluation instances and 148 task types across single-image, text-intensive, and multi-reference editing. Pre-generated checklists shared across models decompose each request into atomic requirements; item-level deductions and visual evidence are established first and then summarized into scores for edit execution, preservation, visual quality, and task-specific correctness. To support the experience-acquisition component of recursive self-improvement (RSI), we develop WeGenEvoStudio, a unified multi-agent harness for RSI-oriented learning that integrates model-conditioned failure diagnosis, retrieval-grounded data construction, supervised adaptation, and re-evaluation. Among 15 evaluated configurations, GPT-Image-2 leads single-image editing with an Overall score of 4.076, while Seedream-5.0-Pro leads text-intensive editing at 4.291. Evolution experiments across four generation and editing benchmarks demonstrate substantial targeted gains: on their report-selected Top-5 single-image targets, FLUX.2 [dev] improves Overall from 1.083 to 3.517, while Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 gains 13.2%. Together, our benchmark and harness provide a foundation for developing more reliable image models through interpretable evaluation and feedback-driven learning.
  </details>

- **[Shifting Focus by Reliability: Geometric Fusion Modulation for Robust RGB-T Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41204)**  `arXiv:2610.41204`  
  _Liuyin Shi, Chengqi Li, Wenbo He_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  RGB-thermal (RGB-T) object detectors combine visible and thermal imagery for robust detection, but signal corruption in either stream can substantially degrade accuracy. Restoration-based methods incur significant latency and do not consistently improve downstream accuracy, raising a more direct question: rather than restoring corrupted inputs, can we efficiently adapt the detector to their varying signal quality? Conventional PEFT offers an efficient route, but its input-independent corrections cannot track diverse test-time corruptions. Our controlled corruption sweeps show that corruption types have similar effects near clean conditions but diverge increasingly with severity, motivating corruption-aware, per-input adaptation. We therefore propose PG-FM (Prompt-Guided Feature Modulation), which adapts RGB-T object detectors to the estimated corruption type and severity of each modality. Under controlled RGB-T corruption simulations, PG-FM preserves clean-set performance, improves mAP50 by up to 20.8 points on FLIR and 19.9 points on KAIST, and transfers without retraining to unmodified, naturally acquired external RGB-T datasets.
  </details>

- **[Beyond CLIP: Calibrated (Few-Shot) Defect Detection With System One Decision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41199)**  `arXiv:2610.41199`  
  _Fadel M. Megahed, Ying-Ju Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cameras and vision sensors are widely used for product and process inspection in manufacturing environments. Traditionally, this process required thousands of images of both nominal and defective products to train such models. Recent research shows that vision transformer (ViT) models, such as CLIP, can reduce this requirement to 50-100 images per nominal and defective class. However, collecting even 50 defective images can be difficult in high-yield or low-volume manufacturing processes. In this paper, we examine the utility of two recently proposed structured decision models (Jev and Clef-Flash) in applications using as few as k=2 reference images per class at inference. In the process, we provide three main contributions: (a) a framework for k-shot learning of these models for image classification/inspection applications; (b) an agentic approach for automatically refining the text descriptions that guide image classification in these decision models; and (c) an approach to calibrate the resulting defect probabilities to improve their reliability for inspection decisions. We test these contributions in five manufacturing case studies. Using two reference images per class, both Jev and Clef-Flash outperform CLIP's test accuracies with the same references (0.75-1.00 [Jev] and 0.71-0.99 [Clef-Flash] vs 0.67-0.84 [CLIP]). Furthermore, we show that our agentic refinement of text descriptions significantly improves accuracy across 6 of 8 model-application combinations, and our calibration approach reduces the Brier score in 13 of the 15 model-application configurations. To support the reproducibility of our work, we provide our replication code and have created the R "decisions" package for evaluating additional models. We also provide a publicly available web app that allows practitioners to inspect their own images with no code.
  </details>

- **[FlowMoDE: Coarse-to-fine Flow Matching for Detail-aware Sim-to-Real Monocular Depth Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41190)**  `arXiv:2610.41190`  
  _Liangjing Shao, Wanhao Liu, Jinsong Lin, Zhiwei Fang, Hongliang Ren_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Monocular depth estimation is a fundamental capability for 3D spatial perception in robotic and intelligent systems. While data-driven discriminative methods have achieved compelling performance for monocular depth estimation, generative approaches can reconstruct fine structural details with stronger generalization. However, most existing generative diffusion models suffer from low inference efficiency caused by multi-step sampling, whereas single-step diffusion models struggle to recover high-fidelity depth maps. To mitigate these drawbacks, this paper presents FlowMoDE, a coarse-to-fine flow-matching-based framework for efficient, structure-aware monocular depth estimation supporting sim-to-real and cross-scene generalization. The core architecture first conducts coarse-level decoding conditioned on diffused features extracted from a pre-trained semantic encoder, and subsequently restores fine-grained structural patterns via expanded decoding layers. Trained exclusively on a single synthetic indoor dataset, our proposed method outperforms prior methods across five real-world indoor and outdoor datasets, yielding an average 14.1% reduction in relative error against state-of-the-art works as well as 9.9\% reduction in chamfer distance for point cloud reconstruction. We further validate the generalization capability of FlowMoDE on realistic robotic scenes, with qualitative comparisons against state-of-the-art methods. The project page isthis https URL
  </details>

- **[MATS: A Multi-Agent Framework for Controllable and Reflective Tumor Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41159)**  `arXiv:2610.41159`  
  _Zhongnan Shi, Keyang Zhou, Siyi Li, Qichao Ying, Xinchen Ma, Shuyi Gong, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthesizing realistic tumors on medical images helps address data scarcity for clinical AI. Existing diffusion-based methods, however, lack fine-grained attribute-level controllability, closed-loop quality verification, and awareness of which morphological patterns are underrepresented in the target distribution. We present MATS, a multi-agent framework for text-controllable, cross-organ tumor synthesis. Given a target disease, MATS first profiles its morphological distribution over a fine-grained attribute space covering tumor geometry and appearance, and constructs a generation plan that amplifies underrepresented subtypes while keeping common ones dominant. It then localizes the target organ, proposes anatomically valid placement, and executes two-stage diffusion to produce the tumor mask and render texture, each conditioned on the corresponding attributes. A closed-loop reflective mechanism verifies attribute alignment at three checkpoints throughout synthesis and provides targeted feedback to guide re-generation when misalignment is detected. Trained exclusively on a single-source pancreatic neuroendocrine tumor dataset, MATS consistently improves downstream segmentation both in-domain and across LiTS, KiTS, and MSD-Pancreas benchmarks, whereas most prior generative augmentation methods fall below real-only training.
  </details>

- **[CompoSET: A Controlled Single-Edit Benchmark for Vision-Language Compositionality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41118)**  `arXiv:2610.41118`  
  _Laure Delisle, Pietro Perona_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language compositionality is the ability to identify which properties, relations, quantities, and states bind to which entities in both an image and a caption, and to match those bindings across modalities. Existing benchmarks evaluate this ability through image-text matching: a model ranks candidate image-caption match similarities and succeeds when the correct match comes on top. In such datasets, paired images differ by more than a single binding: scene, layout, and content all differ, with 97% of pixels changing on average. Such pairs do not isolate compositionality: results are confounded by global image differences. We introduce Compositional Single-EdiT (CompoSET), a benchmark dataset in which each base image-caption pair has an edited counterpart, or foil, that preserves the scene but differs by a single controlled edit, such as color, count, spatial relation, or object state. Base and foil images preserve naturalistic scene complexity yet are visually near-identical: only 8% of pixels change on average. Our dataset has many foils per base scene (~22) in order to separate scene difficulty from edit difficulty, a distinction one-foil-per-base benchmarks cannot make. CompoSET contains 1,776 instances across 80 naturalistic scenes, spanning 16 edit types in 4 semantic categories, with captions at 3 verbosity levels. We test 22 dual-encoder VLMs and find that all models perform at or below chance level on CompoSET, despite scoring well on prior benchmarks. This suggests that apparent progress under prior image-text matching protocols may be an artifact arising from confounds in benchmark design, and that compositional understanding in dual-encoder VLMs is far from solved. CompoSET addresses the confounds and offers a better controlled benchmark to measure future progress.
  </details>

- **[An Empirical Study on Learning Rooftop Solar Potential from Satellite Imagery Directly Without Explicit Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41111)**  `arXiv:2610.41111`  
  _Joshua Lee_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Rooftop solar potential estimation often relies on LiDAR, digital surface models (DSMs), or pipelines that explicitly reconstruct roof geometry before estimating solar potential. We propose Direct Rooftop Solar Prediction (DRSP), an end-to-end formulation that maps satellite imagery and coarse regional sunlight information directly to application-level solar outputs. DRSP neither requires explicit geometric representations as input nor predicts roof orientation, slope, height, DSMs, or other geometric quantities as intermediate outputs, and therefore requires no geometric intermediate supervision. To enable large-scale study of this formulation, we develop a programmatic data-curation pipeline that converts per-panel information from the Google Solar API into usable-rooftop masks and spatial solar-energy targets, yielding two geographically distinct datasets containing approximately 60K rooftops from the U.S. Midwest and 87K from Texas. We demonstrate the feasibility of DRSP and systematically characterize the effects of geographic training distribution, model depth, image resolution, regional sunlight conditioning, and heatmap regression objectives. Overall, our results suggest that useful rooftop-scale solar information can be learned directly from 2D imagery without explicit geometric intermediate representations, offering a simpler path toward large-scale solar assessment. Our code and dataset are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Grounding Image Composition with Label-Conditioned Visual Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41080)**  `arXiv:2610.41080`  
  _Armin Dadras, Tingyu Lin, Robert Sablatnig, Franziska Proksa, Markus Seidl_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image composition labels such as center, diagonal, or rule of thirds describe visual structure rather than object categories. KU-PCP provides image-level labels and class-specific geometric elements, but does not show which visible regions people associate with each of its nine classes. We introduce KU-PCP Elements (KU-PCPE), a label-conditioned region-evidence benchmark for photographic composition. Given an image and a label already judged applicable, the task is to segment the visible regions that explain why the label applies. KU-PCPE contains 4,093 single-annotator training masks and a 1,327-task expert test split with three masks per image-label task. The annotations also include exploratory segment-versus-line ratings that describe whether region or semantic-guideline evidence better explains the label. A held-out expert reaches .867 Dice against the other experts, while the strongest deployable model reaches .664, a proposal oracle .672, and the best vision-language model (VLM) proposal selector .395. KU-PCPE exposes the gap between recognizing a composition label and grounding the human-aligned visual evidence that makes it plausible in a particular image.
  </details>

- **[Single-Annotation, Multi-Hypothesis Segmentation with Semantic-Spatial Residual Banks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41069)**  `arXiv:2610.41069`  
  _Armin Dadras, Robert Sablatnig, Franziska Proksa, Markus Seidl_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-rater segmentation datasets show that a single image can admit several valid masks, yet most training sets expose only one annotation per image. We study single-annotation multi-hypothesis segmentation: each training image exposes one fixed mask, while the model must return a finite bank that covers held-out human annotations at test time. Our experiments reveal a representation tradeoff. Banks learned directly from images recover variations in local boundaries and extent but lose structural hypotheses, whereas frozen semantic features preserve structural grouping but underrepresent spatial variation. We introduce the Semantic-Spatial Residual Bank (SSRB), which freezes a semantic bank trained with balanced assignments and learns a residual from the image for each hypothesis. Across five medical endpoints, SSRB improves coverage of held-out masks at $K=8$ over a Probabilistic U-Net under the same one-mask constraint. On LIDC and LIDC-hard, it also produces substantially more distinct useful masks. On BSDS, it preserves the semantic bank's performance in grouping regions. Its advantage grows as more masks are returned, showing that residuals learned for corresponding heads improve coverage of local annotation variation while preserving how regions are grouped.
  </details>

- **[Segment Anything Pancreas: A Unified MRI Segmentation Model for Pancreatic Cancer Radiotherapy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41063)**  `arXiv:2610.41063`  
  _Zach Eidex, Mojtaba Safari, Kundan Thind, Parag J. Parikh, Zhen Tian, Xiaofeng Yang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Background: Limited tumor annotations and incomplete labels across MRI datasets hinder unified segmentation of pancreatic tumors and organs at risk (OARs) for MRI-guided radiotherapy. Purpose: We propose Pancreas-MedNeXt, a unified model that segments the whole pancreas, its subregions, tumors, and surrounding OARs from one MRI volume. Methods: The model uses a shared MedNeXt encoder-decoder with supervised whole-pancreas and subregion pretraining, followed by multitask fine-tuning. Three output heads predict the pancreatic subregions, tumor, and OARs (stomach, duodenum, kidneys). An annotation-aware loss allows training on partially labeled data without treating unannotated structures as background. Pancreas and tumor were evaluated by three-fold cross-validation on 142 PANTHER patients (92 diagnostic, 50 MR-Linac), subregions and OARs on Cyst-X and AMOS test sets, and pancreas and OARs on 33 external T1-weighted TotalSegmentator scans. Comparisons with nnU-Net, MedNeXt, VISTA3D, and Swin UNETR used the Dice similarity coefficient (DSC) with paired bootstrap 95% CIs. Results: Pancreas-MedNeXt achieved the highest mean pancreas and tumor DSC on diagnostic and MR-Linac MRI. Tumor DSC was 58.41% and 42.55%, exceeding task-specific MedNeXt by 4.94 (95% CI: 0.28-9.45) and 5.89 (95% CI: 2.37-9.72) percentage points, respectively. Whole-pancreas DSC was 85.73% and 70.66%. Pretraining improved head, body, and tail DSC by 2.01, 3.00, and 3.78 percentage points over training from scratch. It also achieved the highest DSC for the pancreas, duodenum, and right kidney on AMOS, and for all five organs on external MRI, exceeding the next-best method by 4.92-8.85 percentagethis http URL: A single Pancreas-MedNeXt model segments tumors, pancreatic subregions, and OARs on diagnostic and MR-Linac MRI, which could streamline contouring for pancreatic cancer radiotherapy.
  </details>

- **[Dreams Are Cheaper Than Flights: Curated World-Model Data Improves Aerial Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41030)**  `arXiv:2610.41030`  
  _Rugved Katole, Vedant Patil, Jenna Kline, Christopher Stewart_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Robot perception is increasingly data driven, yet field data is slow, costly, and sometimes disruptive to collect. Video world models offer a cheaper source: seeded with real frames, they extend a recorded scene into new footage with the deployment's sensor, altitude, and environment. We ask whether such synthetic data matches the utility of real field data and whether detectors trained with it perform in the field. Seeding a 7B-parameter world model with wildlife drone video, we more than double the training set with curated frames. On held-out videos from the same drones, mAP@50--95 rises 4.3\% when these frames are added to real data and 10.3\% when they replace it, and up to 33.5\% on an urban benchmark. Although an expert rejects an estimated 87.5\% of generated clips, the data costs about \$1,100 of GPU time, against more than \$3,200 of field labor alone for a less useful real training set. Synthetic data thus shifts data costs from fieldwork to compute.
  </details>

- **[RADI3N: GeometRic ImAge EDiting via ParametrIc 3D CoNtrol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40973)**  `arXiv:2610.40973`  
  _Abdul Wasi, Akhil Gorugantu, Mahesh Bhosale, Anjan Dutta, Junsong Yuan, Sauradip Nag, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent diffusion-based methods have substantially advanced image editing, yet parametric 3D control at the part level remains challenging. Existing approaches either operate on whole objects with manual rigging, or revert to 2D handles at the part level. They also struggle to model appearance and texture under large transformations, leading to unrealistic outputs in geometry-intensive edits. To address these limitations, we introduce RADI3N, a framework for semantically-grounded part-aware object manipulation with parametric 3D control. Our CS-ARAP deformation module suppresses rotation-field discontinuities at part boundaries via neighborhood covariance smoothing, producing structurally consistent deformations under complex part-level edits. We further introduce an inversion-free selective texture transfer mechanism that suppresses high-frequency positional biases and scales up low-frequency semantic cues within cross-image attention. This eliminates strict spatial bias and supports semantic matching, allowing source appearance to follow the edited part onto its new geometry. Experiments demonstrate that RADI3N achieves state-of-the-art performance in geometric fidelity, viewpoint consistency, and controllability.
  </details>

- **[AVID: A Weakly Aligned RGB-Infrared Dataset and Perturbation Benchmark for Tiny Aerial Targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40949)**  `arXiv:2610.40949`  
  _Dharini Raghavan, Vishnu Rao, Sethu Selvi S_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long-range detection of small drones is complicated by their visual similarity to birds and by the limited detail available from ground-based cameras. Combining visible and infrared observations offers complementary information, but their usefulness depends on target size, cross-modal alignment, and image quality. We present AVID, a benchmark for studying these factors using real RGB-infrared bird-drone surveillance footage originating from the Drone Detection Dataset and curated for the IEEE ICIP 2025 Video and Image Processing Cup. AVID comprises 32,040 corresponding frame pairs from 115 videos, with 35,952 existing bird and drone annotations at $320\times256$ resolution. Targets occupy an unusually narrow size range: 68.1\% have a geometric-mean bounding-box side of 8--16 pixels, and 99.0\% meet the COCO small-object criterion. To characterize the geometric difficulty of combining the modalities, we introduce an object-relative alignment ratio and an alignment burden curve. In a sample of 300 frame pairs, 32.3\% have estimated global translations greater than 5 pixels; among the 264 pairs with matching annotations, 16.3\% exceed one median target side. We further introduce AVID-C, which simulates seven families of image degradation at five severity levels independently in RGB, infrared, or both. A fixed-feature analysis examines how scale, degradation, and added cross-modal shifts affect fitted bird-drone class overlap. At 12--16 pixels, the Bhattacharyya error bounds are 0.148 for RGB, 0.198 for infrared, and 0.046 for their combined features. These values describe separation in the specified feature and density models, not detector accuracy. Together, AVID and AVID-C support scale-aware, alignment-aware, and sensor-specific studies of tiny aerial targets.
  </details>

- **[UFMF: Radar-Guided Kinematic Priors for Real-Time 4D Occupancy Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40947)**  `arXiv:2610.40947`  
  _Zeping Zhang, Zhuoya Zhao, Robert Laganière_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-time 4D occupancy forecasting requires reliably extrapolating both ego motion and surrounding actor motion under strict latency budgets. Recent occupancy world models improve long-horizon fidelity through generative decoders and 3D volumetric modeling, yet their computational cost makes real-time deployment challenging. Light-weight BEV forecasters meet latency requirements but often blur or suppress fast-moving actors at longer horizons. We present UFMF (Ultra-Fast Multi-modal Forecaster), a real-time forecaster that keeps an attention-free diagonal state-space temporal core while injecting targeted kinematic and geometric structure where lightweight models typically fail.
